Output Vollrelation (Schulstandard) oder Kurzfassung (Praxis). Abgrenzung: Vollständige Langfassung siehe vollrelation-langfassung; Familienrichter-Spezifika siehe familienrichter-spezifika. - **relative-abundance-accuracy-assessment**: Use when planning metatranscriptome sequencing or judging whether a sample's depth suffices for accurate abundance measurement, especially of low-abundance transcripts in gut microbiome mRNA data. - **relative-abundance-aggregation-across-loci**: Use when a taxa x time-point read-proportion table from multilocus eDNA amplicon sequencing contains taxa detected at several genetic loci, and you need an amplification-bias-insensitive relative abundance index per taxon per time point. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/relative-abundance-aggregation-across-loci))
- **relative-abundance-calculation**: Use when after generating a frequency count table (e.g., from count_fold_changes output showing numbers of increased vs. decreased metabolites by class) and before creating proportional visualizations (pie charts, stacked bar plots). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/relative-abundance-calculation))
- **relative-abundance-comparison-across-profilers**: Use when benchmarking CAMI-style taxonomic profiles where single-value metrics (F1 score, L1 norm, UniFrac) look equivalent across profilers, and you need to see which clades at class/phylum rank drive abundance differences versus ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/relative-abundance-comparison-across-profilers))
- **relative-abundance-computation-metagenomics**: Use when you need relative abundances for reconstructed full-length SSU rRNA sequences from metagenomics data: map reads with BBMap, sum counts within 97% VSEARCH clusters, and report percent abundances. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/relative-abundance-computation-metagenomics))
- **relative-abundance-estimation-from-read-mappings**: Use when a mock community is pooled from FAST5 read sets and raw signals must be mapped to a combined multi-genome reference to obtain per-genome relative abundance estimates and compare their accuracy to ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/relative-abundance-estimation-from-read-mappings))
- **relative-abundance-normalization**: Use when comparing raw taxonomic profiler profile files (e.g., CAMI profile_submissions and gold-standard profiles) whose relative abundances must be recomputed against the NCBI taxdump before ranking tools by error metrics like the L1 norm. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/relative-abundance-normalization))
- **relative-abundance-normalization**: Use when you have reconstructed full-length SSU rRNA sequences (e.g. metagenome_SSU_sequences.fasta from EMIRGE/MetaRib) plus the original quality-controlled reads and need per-sequence relative abundances for taxonomic profiling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/relative-abundance-normalization))
- **relative-abundance-preprocessing**: Use when when you have raw count tables from 16S rRNA sequencing (microbiome) or LC-MS/MS metabolomics (metabolome) and need to train neural network or regression models for microbe-metabolite relationship prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/relative-abundance-preprocessing))
- **relative-abundance-table-filtering**: Use when a relative abundance table (taxonomy levels l2–l6, e.g. Bangladesh taxtab.rm[[5]]) contains rare taxa that would destabilize downstream GAMLSS-BEZI differential abundance testing or clutter phylum-level mean plots. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/relative-abundance-table-filtering))
- **relative-abundance-threshold-background-removal**: Use when a Progenesis-format LC-MS/MS peak table includes solvent blank or media blank sample groups and features overrepresented in those blanks (e.g. carryover) must be identified and removed before downstream analysis. - **relative-abundance-threshold-filtering**: Use when computing taxon detection sensitivity for 16S amplicon mock communities (e.g., the 136-sample benchmark of DADA2, QIIME 2, Mothur, PathoScope 2, Kraken 2 against SILVA/Greengenes/Kraken Standard/RefSeq libraries) and low-abundance ground-truth taxa must be excluded via a &gt;=0. - **relative-abundance-time-course-data-simulation**: Use when benchmarking an inference method like MetaboPAC that needs ground-truth absolute concentrations — simulate relative-abundance time-course GC-MS-style data from a known metabolic model with controlled noise, sampling frequency, and response-factor distributions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/relative-abundance-time-course-data-simulation))
- **relative-betweenness-normalization**: Use when building a pathway-metabolite interaction network from significant metabolites and you need to rank hub metabolites (e.g. pyruvate, pyruvic acid, arachidonic acid) by normalized centrality rather than raw betweenness values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/relative-betweenness-normalization))

- **relative-error-metrics-computation**: Use when evaluating predicted vs. measured retention times from a chromatographic RT-prediction model (e.g. GNN-RT), to quantify accuracy as mean/median relative error. - **relative-error-quantification-between-codes**: Use when validating that a new solver reproduces a reference code's outputs for matched physical quantities on a common grid, and you need per-quantity maximum-error metrics against documented tolerances.
